# `unsafe` Inventory — root crate

> **Gated.** `tests/unsafe_inventory_gate.rs` lexes every `.rs` under `src/`,
> attributes each code `unsafe` token to its enclosing item, and fails unless
> this file lists exactly those items with exactly those counts and every cell
> filled. Adding, removing or moving an `unsafe` therefore fails CI until the
> row here changes — the inventory edit is part of the pull-request diff, and
> `.github/CODEOWNERS` routes that diff to the maintainer. **Edit this file,
> never the gate, when a count moves.**

**Why an inventory and not a count** (P4-141 criterion 4, GitHub
[#480](https://github.com/developer0hye/libjpeg-turbo-rs/issues/480)). A raw
count says nothing about risk: one precondition-free safe wrapper — P4-135, a
`target_feature` kernel reachable from safe Rust with unvalidated slices —
outweighs hundreds of intrinsic calls behind a checked wrapper. What matters
per site is *why* safe Rust cannot express it, *which invariant* makes it
sound, *whether a safe caller can reach it*, *which test* would catch the
invariant breaking, *which tools* execute it, and *who last read it*. That is
the row.

## Scope and units

- **Scope:** the root crate, `src/` — parser, pipelines, scalar kernels and
  every SIMD backend (`src/simd/`). The C-ABI crate
  (`crates/libjpeg-turbo-rs-capi`, `extern "C"` end to end) has its own
  document, [`UNSAFE_INVENTORY_CAPI.md`](UNSAFE_INVENTORY_CAPI.md), gated by
  the same test; it is complete since 2026-09-09, when `jpeglib.rs` — the
  classic `jpeg_*` surface, the last deferral — landed and the gate's
  `DEFERRED` list emptied, closing criterion 4 in
  `docs/last_mile/phase4.md` under P4-141.
- **Site:** one code `unsafe` token — outside comments, strings and
  literals — as found by the lexer in `tests/helpers/unsafe_scan.rs`. Under
  the crate's `#![deny(unsafe_op_in_unsafe_fn)]`, an `unsafe fn` and the
  `unsafe { … }` block inside it are **two** sites; that is deliberate, so the
  declaration (the contract) and the operation (the risk) are counted apart.
- **Item:** the row key — the enclosing `fn` (the nearest declaration at or
  above the site), or the header for `unsafe impl` / `unsafe trait`. A
  macro-template function is keyed `<macro_rules! name>::$template`, because
  two macros in one file routinely declare the same `$inner`; without the
  prefix, moving an `unsafe` from one to its sibling would change no count.
  Attribution reads a comment-masked copy of the source, so neither a
  commented-out `fn` nor the phrase `unsafe impl …` in a trailing comment can
  name a row. It is a deterministic attribution, not a
  parser: the gate's failure message prints the exact rows it expects.
- **Sections** are one per file, `## \`path\` — N sites`, in path order;
  rows within a section are in first-occurrence order. Sites inside
  `#[cfg(test)]` code are inventoried too and say **test-only** in *Safe
  caller* — a test that dereferences a raw pointer is still code Miri and
  ASan need to see.
- **The `---` rule below is the boundary.** Everything above it is this
  preamble, which explains the format with its own `##` headings and its own
  table and carries no inventory data. Everything below it is read by the
  gate, where a `## ` heading that is not `## \`path\` — N sites` is an
  error rather than prose — so a missing backtick or a stray `1 sites` fails
  CI instead of silently hiding a file.

## Columns

| Column | What goes in it |
|---|---|
| Item | The enclosing item, in backticks. Machine-checked. |
| Sites | Code `unsafe` tokens attributed to the item. Machine-checked. |
| Why not safe Rust | The concrete reason: intrinsics behind `#[target_feature]`, a raw destination pointer plus stride, `from_raw_parts` over owned memory, a hand-rolled once-cell, calling an `unsafe fn` from a safe wrapper. If safe Rust *could* express it at a measured cost, the row says so and names the experiment. |
| Invariant | What must hold — bounds, lifetime, aliasing, CPU feature — and **where it is established** (the `assert!`, the wrapper, the dispatch check). A row whose code carries no SAFETY comment says so. |
| Safe caller | `yes: via …` when a public safe API leads here (naming the checking wrapper), `no: …` when only `unsafe fn` callers inside the crate reach it, or `test-only`. |
| Regression test | The test(s) that would fail if the invariant broke, by function name and file, plus the C cross-validation suite that pins the output. `**none** — …` is an honest answer and a gap to file. |
| Tool coverage | Which CI legs execute the site (vocabulary below), and — just as important — which do not. |
| Reviewer | Who last read the code against the row, and when. |

**What the gate enforces, and what it does not.** It enforces that every
site is *listed*, that each row's count is exact, and that no cell is empty
or a template — so an added, removed or moved `unsafe` cannot merge without
an inventory edit in the same diff. It cannot check that a justification is
*true*: a cell reading `n/a` is complete as far as the parser is concerned.
Content is a review question, which is what `.github/CODEOWNERS` is for.

## Tool-coverage vocabulary

The legs, as configured on 2026-09-08 (`.github/workflows/`):

- **Miri** — `ci.yml` *Miri (non-SIMD unit tests)*: `cargo miri test
  --no-default-features --features std --lib -- --skip simd::`. Root-crate lib
  unit tests only, with the `simd` feature **off** — so nothing under
  `src/simd/` and nothing behind `#[cfg(feature = "simd")]` is ever
  interpreted, and a non-SIMD site is covered only if a lib unit test reaches
  it.
- **ASan / UBSan** — `sanitizers.yml`: `cargo +nightly test --workspace --lib`
  on `ubuntu-latest` x86_64 with `-Z sanitizer=address` and `-Z ub-checks=yes`.
  Lib unit tests only, default features — x86_64 kernels a unit test reaches
  are instrumented; NEON and wasm never are.
- **Unit Tests** — `ci.yml` `cargo test --lib` on Ubuntu, and *Test (ubuntu /
  macos / windows)* — the macOS runner is aarch64, so NEON lib unit tests run
  natively there, uninstrumented.
- **Integration Tests** / **C Interop** — `ci.yml` `cargo test --tests`:
  *Integration Tests* on Ubuntu (x86_64 AVX2), *C Interop* on
  `macos-latest` (aarch64) **only** — its matrix has a single entry, so no
  x86_64 and no Windows leg runs it. Each has a 3.2.0-oracle twin. The
  Windows job builds every test target (`--no-run`) and runs two named
  suites; it runs no C cross-validation, by design.
- **Cross-Arch SIMD** — `cross-arch.yml`: Linux aarch64 NEON `--tests`; Linux
  x86_64 AVX2 `--tests`; AVX2 disabled at compile time `--tests`; **no-AVX2
  emulated** — `--lib` plus four integration binaries under `qemu-x86_64 -cpu
  Nehalem` (no AVX, so the runtime-dispatch fallback arms execute); and the
  WASM SIMD128 smoke (wasm-bindgen tests on Node).
- **WASM (wasip1)** — `wasm.yml` `cargo test --target wasm32-wasip1` under
  wasmtime, `+simd128` forced by `.cargo/config.toml`, lib and integration.
- **ARMv7 (scalar, qemu)** — `armv7.yml`: 32-bit, no NEON backend; the
  non-SIMD sites in 32-bit.
- **Fuzz Smoke** — `fuzz-smoke.yml`: thirteen cargo-fuzz targets (libFuzzer with
  ASan), x86_64 Linux, six-hourly.
- **Full C Parity** — `full-c-parity.yml`: the exhaustive C matrices, on
  schedule or dispatch, x86_64 and aarch64.

## Maintaining a row

1. Run `cargo test --test unsafe_inventory_gate`. The failure names the file,
   the item and the count it found, with the source lines, and prints a
   paste-ready section for a file that is new here.
2. Re-read the code, not just the diff: a changed count means the contract
   may have changed. Update every cell that the change touches, and the
   *Reviewer* cell always.
3. If the honest *Regression test* cell is `**none**`, file the gap in
   `docs/last_mile/phase4.md` before the pull request merges. The
   thirty-two rows that answer `**none**` today are already filed, together,
   as P4-191 ([#609](https://github.com/developer0hye/libjpeg-turbo-rs/issues/609));
   add to that item rather than opening a sibling.

---

| Item | Sites | Why not safe Rust | Invariant | Safe caller | Regression test | Tool coverage | Reviewer |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `output` | 1 | Takes `as_mut_ptr().add(dst_offset)` into the component plane and hands the raw pointer plus `ci.comp_w` stride to the private `unsafe fn idct_islow_strided` so each 8x8 block lands in place without a 64-byte temp copy. | No SAFETY comment on the block; the invariant is that the plane holds `checked_span([comp_w, blocks_y, 8])` bytes with `comp_w = mcus_x * h_samp * 8`, and `bx < blocks_x = mcus_x * h_samp`, `by < blocks_y`, so `by*8*comp_w + bx*8 + 7*comp_w + 8 <= plane.len()`. P4-136 replaced the former `set_len` on an uninitialised `Vec` here. | yes: via `ProgressiveDecoder::output` and `ProgressiveDecoder::finish` (`pub fn`); geometry is fixed in `ProgressiveDecoder::with_limits` from the parsed SOF. | `progressive_output_path_is_miri_covered` (src/api/progressive_output.rs), `c_djpeg_progressive_output_final_diff_zero` and `c_djpeg_progressive_intermediate_diff_zero` (tests/progressive_output.rs), `c_xval_progressive_final_scan_420` (tests/cross_check_progressive_scans.rs). | Miri via `progressive_output_path_is_miri_covered` (generic arm, `simd` off); ASan/UBSan + Unit Tests via the same test (AVX2 arm on ubuntu); Test (macos) = NEON arm; Integration Tests + C Interop (macos) + Cross-Arch NEON/AVX2; WASM lib+integration; ARMv7 lib; Fuzz Smoke (fuzz_progressive_decoder). | P4-141 c4 landing, 2026-09-08 (source read; no independent human review yet) |
| `idct_islow_strided` | 2 | Raw destination pointer + stride for a strided IDCT write: dispatches to `neon_idct_islow_strided` / `avx2_idct_islow_strided` / `sse2_idct_islow_strided`, else IDCTs into a 64-byte temp and `copy_nonoverlapping`s 8 rows at `output.add(row * stride)`. | SAFETY doc: "`output` must point to at least `7 * stride + 8` writable bytes". CPU feature: `cpu_has!("avx2")` / `cpu_has!("sse2")` checked at the call under `cfg(target_arch = "x86_64", feature = "simd")`; NEON is mandatory on aarch64. Duplicates `Decoder::idct_islow_strided` in `pipeline_impl/color.rs` minus the `DctMethod` branch. | no: private `unsafe fn`; its only caller is `ProgressiveDecoder::output` in this file, which derives the pointer from a `checked_span`-sized plane. | `progressive_output_path_is_miri_covered` (src/api/progressive_output.rs), `c_djpeg_progressive_output_final_diff_zero` (tests/progressive_output.rs), `c_xval_progressive_grayscale` and `c_xval_progressive_final_scan_444` (tests/cross_check_progressive_scans.rs). | Miri via `progressive_output_path_is_miri_covered` (generic arm only, `simd` off); ASan/UBSan + Unit Tests (AVX2 arm); Test (macos) NEON arm; emulated Nehalem leg = SSE2 arm, via the `--lib` half that carries `progressive_output_path_is_miri_covered` (the compile-time no-AVX2 leg does **not** reach it — dispatch is a runtime CPUID query that ignores `-C target-feature`, #320); WASM lib+integration (generic arm); ARMv7 lib; Fuzz Smoke (fuzz_progressive_decoder). | P4-141 c4 landing, 2026-09-08 (source read; no independent human review yet) |

## `src/common/huffman_table.rs` — 6 sites

| Item | Sites | Why not safe Rust | Invariant | Safe caller | Regression test | Tool coverage | Reviewer |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `impl Sync for OnceBox` | 1 | `unsafe impl Sync` for a hand-rolled `no_std` once-cell (issue #356): `OnceBox<T>` wraps an `AtomicPtr<T>` and hands out `&'static T`, which the compiler cannot prove shareable across threads on its own. | SAFETY comment: "the pointee is published exactly once via a Release CAS and only ever read through an Acquire load, so all readers observe a fully-initialised value. T must be shareable for `&'static T` to be." Enforced by the `T: Send + Sync + 'static` bound; `get_or_init` is the only writer. P4-141 criterion 6's named blocker. | yes: via `std_huffman_tables()` (`pub fn`), reached from `Decoder::new` through `fill_default_huffman_tables` (src/decode/pipeline_impl/api.rs) for every non-progressive stream. | `tests_ac_max_k::ac_first_records_highest_written_zigzag_index` (src/decode/progressive.rs, calls `std_huffman_tables()` directly); `baseline_sos_referencing_undefined_dht_decodes` (tests/huffman_table_defaults.rs). **none** races the first init: `ten_threads_decode_same_jpeg_all_correct` (tests/concurrency.rs) decodes on the main thread before spawning. | Non-SIMD, so Miri DOES cover it via `tests_ac_max_k::*` (root `--lib`), single-threaded only; ASan/UBSan + Unit Tests + Test (all three); ARMv7 lib; WASM lib; every integration leg through `Decoder::new`; Fuzz Smoke (fuzz_decompress). No leg exercises a concurrent first initialisation. | P4-141 c4 landing, 2026-09-08 (source read; no independent human review yet) |
| `impl Send for OnceBox` | 1 | `unsafe impl Send` for the same once-cell: the struct owns a leaked `Box<T>` only through a raw `AtomicPtr`, so `Send` is not auto-derived. | Same SAFETY comment as `Sync` (lines 250-252): the only state is the atomic pointer, the pointee is `T: Send + Sync + 'static` and is never freed once published, so moving the cell between threads moves nothing that is thread-affine. In practice the only instance is the `static STD_TABLES` in `std_huffman_tables`, which is never moved. | yes: via `std_huffman_tables()` (`pub fn`); the `static` requires `Sync`, and `Send` is only needed for the bound to be coherent. | `tests_ac_max_k::refine_eob_walk_bounded_by_max_k_is_bit_identical` (src/decode/progressive.rs); `issue_384_decoder_decodes_on_another_thread` (tests/regression_issue_384_decoder_send.rs) reads the published tables from a second thread. | Non-SIMD: Miri via `tests_ac_max_k::*` (single-threaded); ASan/UBSan + Unit Tests + Test (all three); ARMv7 lib; WASM lib; all integration legs. No leg moves an `OnceBox` across threads because the only instance is a `static`. | P4-141 c4 landing, 2026-09-08 (source read; no independent human review yet) |
| `get_or_init` | 4 | Lazy one-time publication without `std::sync::OnceLock`: four raw derefs of a leaked `Box` pointer (`&*existing`, `&*boxed`, `&*winner`) and one `Box::from_raw` to free the losing initialiser's value. | SAFETY comments on each site. A non-null `Acquire` load means a prior `AcqRel` CAS from `null` published a `Box::into_raw` pointer that is never freed, so `&'static T` is valid; the winner returns its own pointer, the loser (`Err(winner)`) frees only its never-published `boxed` and returns the winner's. `init` may run twice, so it must be side-effect-free. | yes: via `std_huffman_tables()` (`pub fn`); `get_or_init` itself is a private method of a private type. | `tests_ac_max_k::ac_first_records_highest_written_zigzag_index` and `refine_placed_nonzero_extends_max_k` (src/decode/progressive.rs) cover first-init and fast paths; `baseline_sos_referencing_undefined_dht_decodes` (tests/huffman_table_defaults.rs). **none** reaches the `Err(winner)` arm — needs two threads racing the first call. | Non-SIMD: Miri via `tests_ac_max_k::*` (root `--lib`), which covers the null-then-publish path and the Acquire fast path but not the losing CAS arm; ASan/UBSan + Unit Tests + Test (all three); ARMv7 lib; WASM lib; every decode integration leg and Fuzz Smoke target that builds a `Decoder`. | P4-141 c4 landing, 2026-09-08 (source read; no independent human review yet) |

## `src/decode/pipeline_impl/arithmetic.rs` — 5 sites

| Item | Sites | Why not safe Rust | Invariant | Safe caller | Regression test | Tool coverage | Reviewer |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## `src/encode/pipeline_impl/baseline.rs` — 2 sites

| Item | Sites | Why not safe Rust | Invariant | Safe caller | Regression test | Tool coverage | Reviewer |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `compress_with_params` | 2 | Site 1 calls the `unsafe fn` `avx2_downsample_h2v2_plane` (`#[target_feature(enable = "avx2")]`) to fold the Cb/Cr row strip into half-res buffers. Site 2 hoists the `BitWriter` cursor (`begin_block`/`encode_block_hoisted`/`end_block`, raw `*mut u8`) across a whole 4:2:0 MCU row and feeds `avx2_extract_fdct_quantize` raw plane pointers, one reserve per row instead of per MCU. | No SAFETY comment on either block. ## `src/simd/wasm32/idct.rs` — 2 sites

| Item | Sites | Why not safe Rust | Invariant | Safe caller | Regression test | Tool coverage | Reviewer |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
